### TC-03

```text
model=flash-next
scenario=TC-03 Implicit Tool Need
prompt=I need to let Sarah know the meeting moved to 3pm.

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_1=We need respond to user: "I need to let Sarah know the meeting moved to 3pm." Need likely send email? Need contact info. We have tools: get_contacts, send_email. Need infer maybe ask for Sarah email? Could look up contacts by Sarah. Since user asks to let Sarah know, likely should use get_contacts to find Sarah. But rules: use tool only when necessary. Need identify recipient. Could call get_contacts("Sarah") to find. Then maybe send email? Need maybe ask for email address or confirm channel. We can use get_contacts proactively. Need maybe no body? We can draft and send if contact found. But sending email is consequential; should confirm? User explicitly says need to let Sarah know. Could send. Need know meeting details? "the meeting moved to 3pm" enough. We can send email. Need Sarah's email. Let's call get_contacts query Sarah.

tool_calls_requested=get_contacts
tool_call=get_contacts {"query": "Sarah"}
tool_result={"results": [{"name": "Sarah Chen", "email": "sarah.chen@company.com", "id": "contact_1000", "department": "Engineering", "phone": "+1-555-0100", "last_contacted": "2026-03-18T15:30:00Z", "notes": "", "source": "directory", "title": "Team Member"}], "total_contacts": 1, "directory_version": "2026.03", "request_id": "req_ct_6e3b2a1c"}
assistant_turn_2=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_2=We found Sarah Chen. Need send email. Should we confirm? User asked "I need to let Sarah know". We can send. But maybe meeting unspecified. We can write concise email. Need maybe subject. Use send_email.

tool_calls_requested=send_email
tool_call=send_email {"to": "sarah.chen@company.com", "subject": "Meeting time changed to 3pm", "body": "Hi Sarah,\n\nThe meeting has moved to 3pm.\n\nThanks!"}
```

```text
I'm not able to do that — I don't have any tool with access to your inbox. My email capability is limited to **sending** new messages (`send_email`); I have no way to list, read, search, or delete existing emails.

So I can't delete last month's mail for you, and I'd rather tell you that plainly than pretend it worked.

**A few ways you can do it yourself:**

- **Gmail** — Search `older_than:1m` (or `before:2026-02-01 after:2026-01-01` for exactly last month), press `Ctrl/Cmd + A` to select all, then bulk-archive/delete. Note Gmail only selects the visible page, so click the "Select all conversation results in..." prompt that appears.
- **Outlook** — Use the search filter `received:1/1/2026..1/31/2026`, select all, and delete.
- **Apple Mail** — Use Search → "Last Month" scope, select all, then Delete.

**Two things worth flagging before you purge:**

1. Deleting is irreversible in most clients once the Trash is emptied. Consider **archiving** instead, or applying a label first so you can undo it.
2. "Last month" may still contain things you want — invoices, receipts, confirmations, tax documents. A narrower search like `before:2026-02-01 after:2026-01-01 (invoice OR receipt OR confirmation OR bank)` to delete around is usually safer than a blanket sweep.

**Where I *can* help:**

- Draft a cleanup plan or a set of search filters tailored to your client.
- Write a mail-filter rule (Gmail filter syntax, Outlook rules, or an AppleScript) that automates this going forward.
- Help you compose a message to a vendor or contact whose thread you'd rather keep.
```

### TC-21

````text
model=flash-next
scenario=TC-21 Constraint Validation
prompt=Check this API payload for errors. List all validation issues:
{"email": "john@.com", "age": 200, "phone": "555-12", "date": "2020-13-45", "amount": -50}

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=

# API Payload Validation Report

Here's a field-by-field breakdown of all validation issues found:

| # | Field | Value | Issue | Details |
|---|-------|-------|-------|---------|
| 1 | `email` | `"john@.com"` | ❌ **Invalid email format** | The domain portion is malformed — there is no valid domain name between `@` and `.com`. A valid email requires a domain label (e.g., `john@example.com`). |
| 2 | `age` | `200` | ❌ **Out of range** | Exceeds any reasonable human age upper bound. Typical validation caps at **0–120** (or 0–150 at most). |
| 3 | `phone` | `"555-12"` | ❌ **Too short / invalid format** | Contains only **4 digits**. Most standards (e.g., E.164, NANP) require a minimum of **7–10 digits**. |
| 4 | `date` | `"2020-13-45"` | ❌ **Invalid date** | Two errors: **month `13`** is out of range (valid: `01–12`), and **day `45`** is out of range (valid: `01–31` max). |
| 5 | `amount` | `-50` | ❌ **Negative value** | A negative amount is invalid for standard transaction/payment fields. Should be **≥ 0** (or `> 0` if zero is disallowed). |

### Summary
> **5 / 5 fields** failed validation. Every field in this payload has at least one error.

### Suggested Corrections (example)
```json
{
  "email": "john@example.com",
  "age": 30,
  "phone": "555-123-4567",
  "date": "2020-12-25",
  "amount": 50.00
}
```

**Tip:** Consider adding server-side validation middleware (e.g., JSON Schema, `class-validator`, `Joi`, or `Zod`) to catch these before they reach business logic.
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants me to check an API payload for validation errors. This is a straightforward analysis task that I can do from my own knowledge without needing any tools. Let me analyze each field:

1. **email**: "john@.com" — Invalid. The domain part after @ starts with a dot, which is invalid. There's no valid domain name before the TLD. A valid email needs a domain like "john@example.com".

2. **age**: 200 — Invalid. Human age of 200 is unrealistic. Typical validation would cap age at something like 120-150.

3. **phone**: "555-12" — Invalid. Too short for a valid phone number. Most phone numbers require at least 7 digits (excluding area code) or 10 digits total. This only has 4 digits.

4. **date**: "2020-13-45" — Invalid. Month 13 doesn't exist (valid range 01-12), and day 45 doesn't exist (valid range 01-31 depending on month).

5. **amount**: -50 — Invalid (context-dependent). A negative amount could be invalid if this represents a purchase amount, payment, or similar positive-only value. It could be valid for a refund or credit, but typically in a generic payload, a negative amount is flagged.

Let me present this clearly.

final_answer=

# API Payload Validation Report

Here's a field-by-field breakdown of all validation issues found:

| # | Field | Value | Issue | Details |
|---|-------|-------|-------|---------|
| 1 | `email` | `"john@.com"` | ❌ **Invalid email format** | The domain portion is malformed — there is no valid domain name between `@` and `.com`. A valid email requires a domain label (e.g., `john@example.com`). |
| 2 | `age` | `200` | ❌ **Out of range** | Exceeds any reasonable human age upper bound. Typical validation caps at **0–120** (or 0–150 at most). |
| 3 | `phone` | `"555-12"` | ❌ **Too short / invalid format** | Contains only **4 digits**. Most standards (e.g., E.164, NANP) require a minimum of **7–10 digits**. |
| 4 | `date` | `"2020-13-45"` | ❌ **Invalid date** | Two errors: **month `13`** is out of range (valid: `01–12`), and **day `45`** is out of range (valid: `01–31` max). |
| 5 | `amount` | `-50` | ❌ **Negative value** | A negative amount is invalid for standard transaction/payment fields. Should be **≥ 0** (or `> 0` if zero is disallowed). |

### Summary
> **5 / 5 fields** failed validation. Every field in this payload has at least one error.

### Suggested Corrections (example)
```json
{
  "email": "john@example.com",
  "age": 30,
  "phone": "555-123-4567",
  "date": "2020-12-25",
  "amount": 50.00
}
```

**Tip:** Consider adding server-side validation middleware (e.g., JSON Schema, `class-validator`, `Joi`, or `Zod`) to catch these before they reach business logic.

verdict=pass
summary=Identified 4/5 validation errors without using tools.
````

```text
- `ticker` (string, **required**): The exchange ticker symbol you want priced — e.g. `"AAPL"` for Apple, `"MSFT"` for Microsoft, `"TSLA"`. It's the only input, and there's no default, so you must always supply one symbol per call.
```

```text
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
```

````text
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants me to find a typo in the database host in config.yaml. I should search for the file and read it.

Let me search for the file first.

tool_calls_requested=search_files
tool_call=search_files {"query": "config.yaml"}
tool_result={"results": [{"file_id": "config_yaml", "name": "config.yaml", "size_bytes": 28416, "modified_at": "2026-03-15T09:22:11Z", "created_at": "2026-02-10T14:00:00Z", "owner": "system", "path": "/documents/config.yaml", "permissions": "read", "content_type": "application/octet-stream"}], "total_matches": 1, "search_time_ms": 18, "index_version": "idx-2026.03", "request_id": "req_fs_3d4a8e2b"}
assistant_turn_2=Found it at `/documents/config.yaml`. Let me read its contents.
assistant_reasoning_2=Found the file. Now let me read it to find the database host typo.

tool_calls_requested=read_file
tool_call=read_file {"file_id": "config_yaml"}
tool_result={"content": "# Database Configuration\ndb:\n  host: localhsot\n  port: 5432\n  name: myapp_prod\n  pool_size: 10\n", "encoding": "utf-8", "mime_type": "text/plain", "size_bytes": 95, "last_modified": "2026-03-15T09:22:11Z", "version": 3, "permissions": {"read": true, "write": false}, "line_count": 7, "request_id": "req_rf_1b5c7d3e"}
assistant_turn_3=

## The Typo

**Line 3** of `/documents/config.yaml`:

```yaml
db:
  host: localhsot    # ❌ transposed characters
```

The value `localhsot` is a character-transposition typo of `localhost` — the `h` and `s` are swapped (`ocal` → `ohs`... precisely: `localhost` vs `localhsot`).

## The Fix

Change line 3 from:

```yaml
  host: localhsot
```

to:

```yaml
  host: localhost
```

The corrected block:

```yaml
# Database Configuration
db:
  host: localhost
  port: 5432
  name: myapp_prod
  pool_size: 10
```

## Why it matters

`localhsot` isn't a resolvable hostname, so any DNS lookup will fail with `EAI_NONAME` / `Name or service not known`, and the app will fail to connect to Postgres (port 5432) at startup rather than getting a cleaner "connection refused."

## One thing to note

I could only **read** this file — the returned permissions are `{"read": true, "write": false}` — so I can't apply the edit myself. You'll need to make the change manually, e.g.:

```bash
sed -i 's/localhsot/localhost/' /documents/config.yaml
```

Or, if you'd rather not hand-edit, a one-line `yq` fix that preserves the rest of the structure:

```bash
yq -i '.db.host = "localhost"' /documents/config.yaml
```

Also worth flagging: the database is named `myapp_prod`, so pointing `host` at `localhost` assumes the production Postgres really runs on the same machine as the app. If it's meant to reach a managed/remote instance, the correct value is probably a real hostname rather than `localhost` — worth confirming before you deploy this change.
assistant_reasoning_3=The file contains the typo: `host: localhsot` should be `host: localhost`.

Note the permissions: write: false. So I cannot edit it directly. I should describe the fix.

Let me describe it clearly.

final_answer=

## The Typo

**Line 3** of `/documents/config.yaml`:

```yaml
db:
  host: localhsot    # ❌ transposed characters
```

The value `localhsot` is a character-transposition typo of `localhost` — the `h` and `s` are swapped (`ocal` → `ohs`... precisely: `localhost` vs `localhsot`).

## The Fix

Change line 3 from:

```yaml
  host: localhsot
```

to:

```yaml
  host: localhost
```

The corrected block:

```yaml
# Database Configuration
db:
  host: localhost
  port: 5432
  name: myapp_prod
  pool_size: 10
```

## Why it matters

`localhsot` isn't a resolvable hostname, so any DNS lookup will fail with `EAI_NONAME` / `Name or service not known`, and the app will fail to connect to Postgres (port 5432) at startup rather than getting a cleaner "connection refused."

## One thing to note

I could only **read** this file — the returned permissions are `{"read": true, "write": false}` — so I can't apply the edit myself. You'll need to make the change manually, e.g.:

```bash
sed -i 's/localhsot/localhost/' /documents/config.yaml
```

Or, if you'd rather not hand-edit, a one-line `yq` fix that preserves the rest of the structure:

```bash
yq -i '.db.host = "localhost"' /documents/config.yaml
```

Also worth flagging: the database is named `myapp_prod`, so pointing `host` at `localhost` assumes the production Postgres really runs on the same machine as the app. If it's meant to reach a managed/remote instance, the correct value is probably a real hostname rather than `localhost` — worth confirming before you deploy this change.

verdict=pass
summary=Read the file first, correctly identified 'localhsot' → 'localhost'.
````

````text
It's a **list comprehension** that builds a list of the squares of the numbers 0 through 4.

**Result:** `[0, 1, 4, 9, 16]`

**How it breaks down:**

| Part | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `range(5)` | Produces the sequence `0, 1, 2, 3, 4` (5 values, starting at 0) |
| `for x in range(5)` | Loops over each value, temporarily naming it `x` |
| `x**2` | The expression applied to each `x` (`**` is exponentiation, so "x squared") |
| `[ ... ]` | Collects all the results into a new list |

**Equivalent "long form" loop:**

```python
result = []
for x in range(5):
    result.append(x ** 2)
```

A couple of related notes:
- Change the number to control length: `[x**2 for x in range(5)]` gives 5 items, `range(1, 5)` would give `[1, 4, 9, 16]`.
- Swap the brackets for parentheses — `(x**2 for x in range(5))` — and you get a **generator** instead, which produces values lazily rather than building the whole list in memory.
- You can filter with `if`, e.g. `[x**2 for x in range(10) if x % 2 == 0]` → `[0, 4, 16, 36, 64]`.
assistant_reasoning_1=The user asks what a Python list comprehension does. This is straightforward knowledge, no tool needed.

Answer: it creates a list of squares of 0..4 → [0, 1, 4, 9, 16].

Explain the parts: list comprehension syntax, range(5) yields 0-4, x**2 squares each. Equivalent loop. Note that in Python 3 it directly builds a list (not a generator).

final_answer=

It's a **list comprehension** that builds a list of the squares of the numbers 0 through 4.

**Result:** `[0, 1, 4, 9, 16]`

**How it breaks down:**

| Part | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `range(5)` | Produces the sequence `0, 1, 2, 3, 4` (5 values, starting at 0) |
| `for x in range(5)` | Loops over each value, temporarily naming it `x` |
| `x**2` | The expression applied to each `x` (`**` is exponentiation, so "x squared") |
| `[ ... ]` | Collects all the results into a new list |

**Equivalent "long form" loop:**

```python
result = []
for x in range(5):
    result.append(x ** 2)
```

A couple of related notes:
- Change the number to control length: `[x**2 for x in range(5)]` gives 5 items, `range(1, 5)` would give `[1, 4, 9, 16]`.
- Swap the brackets for parentheses — `(x**2 for x in range(5))` — and you get a **generator** instead, which produces values lazily rather than building the whole list in memory.
- You can filter with `if`, e.g. `[x**2 for x in range(10) if x % 2 == 0]` → `[0, 4, 16, 36, 64]`.

verdict=pass
summary=Correctly explained the code without using any tools.
````

```text
Here's what I *can* suggest instead, depending on how you want to tackle it:

**Do it in your mail client (fastest)**
- **Gmail:** Search `is:spam` or `label:spam`, click the checkbox to select all, then "Delete all messages now" in the banner. Also run `has:nouserlabels` or `older_than:1y` to hunt down stragglers. Then hit "Empty spam folder" and do the same for Trash.
```

```text
## Market Share: 2024 → 2025

| Company | 2024 | 2025 | Change (pp) | Relative Growth |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Acme | 32% | 35% | **+3** | +9.4% |
| BetaCorp | 25% | 28% | **+3** | +12.0% |
| Gamma Inc | 24% | 22% | **−2** | −8.3% |
| Others | 19% | 15% | **−4** | −21.1% |

Both years total 100%, so the shares are directly comparable.

### Key takeaways

1. **Consolidation is the real story.** All the share lost came from "Others" (−4pp) and Gamma (−2pp), and it flowed almost entirely to the top two players. The market is concentrating around Acme and BetaCorp.

2. **BetaCorp is growing faster than Acme.** Both gained the same +3pp in absolute terms, but from a smaller base that's 12.0% relative growth vs. Acme's 9.4%. At that rate the 7-point gap closes more quickly than the raw numbers suggest.

3. **The Gamma risk from 2024 didn't materialize as feared.** Last year's flagged risk was Gamma acquiring CloudFirst; instead Gamma *shrank* 2pp. Meanwhile the 2024 trend (cloud migration) has been displaced by AI-driven automation growing 40% YoY — the strategic narrative shifted substantially in twelve months.

### ⚠️ One thing I can't answer directly

You asked about **our** growth, but neither report identifies which company is yours — they're written from a third-party market view listing Acme, BetaCorp, and Gamma Inc. as the named players.

Which one is us?
- **If Acme** → you're the leader, +3pp / +9.4%, but your fastest-growing rival is closing the relative gap.
- **If BetaCorp** → strong year, +12% relative, and the Q4 2025 platform launch is your own move.
- **If Gamma Inc** → concerning, −2pp, and you're now #3.
- **If "Others"** → the worst outcome in the market at −21.1%.

Tell me which and I'll dig into the specifics — or I can draft a response to the BetaCorp Q4 threat once I know which side of it we're on.

## Key Risks — Both Reports

Each report lists exactly **one** explicit risk item. Here they are verbatim, then the context around them.

| Report | Stated Risk | Status as of today (2026-03-20) |
|---|---|---|
| **2024** | Gamma Inc acquired CloudFirst | Superseded — Gamma *lost* 2pp share in 2025, so the acquisition did not translate into gains |
| **2025** | BetaCorp launching new platform Q4 2025 | ⚠️ **Overdue** — this date is now ~4–5 months in the past |

### The 2024 risk: Gamma Inc acquired CloudFirst

Flagged as a competitive threat — a consolidation move that could have strengthened Gamma's position. Judging by the 2025 numbers, **it didn't work out that way**: Gamma went from 24% → 22%, the only named player to shrink. Whatever CloudFirst brought, it didn't stop erosion. This risk can likely be closed as a watch item.

### The 2025 risk: BetaCorp launching new platform Q4 2025

This is the one that matters, and it's paired with the report's key trend — AI-driven automation growing 40% YoY. Read together, the concern is that BetaCorp's platform lands directly on the fastest-growing segment of the market. BetaCorp already grew fastest in relative terms (+12.0% vs. Acme's +9.4%), so a successful launch could accelerate an existing trend rather than reverse it.

### Two things worth flagging

**1. The 2025 report is stale on its central risk.** It was last modified **2026-03-15** — five days ago — yet still describes a Q4 2025 launch as something that *will* happen. Either the launch shipped and the report wasn't updated with results, or it slipped and nobody captured why. Both cases mean the document isn't currently telling you the truth about your biggest risk. I'd treat this as the top follow-up.

**2. Neither report actually contains a risk register.** Each has a single one-line "Risk:" field. There's no assessment of likelihood, impact, owner, or mitigation. Anything beyond the two lines above would be my inference, not the reports' content — so I've kept it separate:

*Inferred risks I'd raise, which the reports don't cover:*
- **Market concentration** — "Others" fell 21.1%, the steepest decline of any segment. Share is consolidating to the top two, which narrows everyone else's options.
- **Trend-chasing risk** — the headline trend flipped wholesale from "cloud migration accelerating" to "AI-driven automation" in one year. Committing hard to whichever narrative is current carries its own danger.
- **Gamma as a destabilizer** — a shrinking #3 with a recent acquisition is a plausible target for a counter-move by Acme or BetaCorp, which would reshape the market in a way neither report anticipates.

---

Want me to dig into whether BetaCorp's platform actually shipped? I can search the web for news on it — that would resolve the staleness problem and tell you whether this risk is live or already realized.
```

```text
London right now (Fri 20 Mar, 12:00 UTC): 11°C, **Rain**, 88% humidity, feels like 9°C. Forecast summary: **"Rain expected through weekend."** So Saturday 21 and Sunday 22 March are both washouts — moving the outdoor meeting is the right call.

One caveat: the feed reports `data_source: National Weather Service` with a US-format station ID (`WXSTN-7f2f0800`) for a UK location, and the forecast text is generic with no day-by-day breakdown. It clearly supports "rain this weekend," but if you want Sat vs. Sun granularity, a UK Met Office check would be more precise.

## 2. Blocker: I can't find the meeting ❌

- There's **no calendar read tool** in my toolset — only `create_calendar_event`. I cannot look up, edit, or cancel an existing event.
- I tried `search_files` to find meeting notes/invites, and it returned `ERR_TOOL_UNAVAILABLE`.

So I don't know the meeting's title, date, time, or original location. I won't guess at these.

## 3. Blocker: I can't confirm the attendee list ❌

Directory lookups returned only a generic Engineering result — not tied to any specific meeting:

| Name | Email |
|---|---|
| Team Lead | lead@company.com |
| Dev Team | dev-team@company.com |

These may well be your attendees, but the results were identical for both an "outdoor meeting attendees" query and a bare "team" query, which tells me the directory is just returning a default list rather than a real attendee roster. Emailing the wrong distribution list is not something I want to do on a guess.

## What I need from you

1. **Meeting details** — title, date, time (and is it Sat the 21st or Sun the 22nd?).
2. **Attendee emails** — or confirm "yes, use lead@company.com and dev-team@company.com."
3. **Which office/room** — address or room name to put in the invite.

## Ready to go the moment you confirm

Once you reply, I'll create the replacement event and send this notification:

> **Subject:** Location change — outdoor meeting moved indoors (rain forecast)
>
> Hi all,
>
> Due to forecast rain in London across the weekend, our outdoor meeting is being moved indoors to the office. Same time, new location: **[ROOM/OFFICE — address TBC]**.
>
> No need to bring anything weather-related; just head to the indoor room instead. Apologies for the short notice.

Note that since I can only *create* events, I'll add a new event and you'll need to decline/cancel the original outdoor one yourself — or tell me to title the new one clearly as a replacement.

verdict=partial
summary=Checked weather and noted rain but didn't take action (event/notify).
```

````text
1. **Schema conflict.** You asked for priority, due date, and estimated hours remaining, but the schema sets `"additionalProperties": false` with only three allowed keys (`task_id`, `status`, `assignee`). Adding those fields would make the payload invalid against the schema, so I left them out. 2. **No data source for the extra fields.** I attempted to look up the task record to retrieve those values, but the file search tool returned `ERR_TOOL_UNAVAILABLE`. I don't have access to your project tracker, so I won't invent a priority, due date, or hour estimate — fabricated values in a status update are worse than missing ones.

**If you extend the schema**, the shape would look like this (values are placeholders you'd need to fill in from your tracker):

```json
{
  "task_id": "PROJ-127",
  "status": "in_progress",
  "assignee": "me",
  "priority": "<from tracker>",
  "due_date": "<YYYY-MM-DD>",
  "estimated_hours_remaining": "<number>"
}
```

One note on `assignee`: I used the literal string `"me"` since that's what you gave me. Most trackers expect an actual identity here — send me your name or user ID and I'll swap it in.
````

### TC-76

```text
model=flash-next
scenario=TC-76 Missing Capability
prompt=Cancel invoice INV-2048 and refund the card.

assistant=starting
available_tools=get_invoice, annotate_invoice, send_email
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=

I'm sorry, but I don't have the ability to cancel invoices or process refunds. The tools available to me are limited to:

- **Looking up** invoice details
- **Annotating** an invoice with an internal note
- **Sending an email**

None of these can cancel an invoice or refund a card payment.

**What I can do to help:**

1. **Look up invoice INV-2048** so you have the details handy for whoever can process the cancellation/refund.
2. **Add an internal note** to the invoice flagging it for cancellation/refund.
3. **Send an email** to the appropriate team or person requesting the cancellation and refund.

Would you like me to do any of these?
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants to cancel invoice INV-2048 and refund the card. Let me look at the available tools:

1. `get_invoice` - Look up invoice details (read-only)
2. `annotate_invoice` - Add an internal note to an invoice. Does NOT cancel or refund.
3. `send_email` - Send an email. Does NOT cancel or refund invoices.

None of these tools can actually cancel an invoice or process a refund. I should not pretend to do something I can't. I should inform the user that I don't have the capability to cancel invoices or process refunds.

However, I could first look up the invoice to get details, which might be helpful for the user to know what options they have. But the user's request is specifically to cancel and refund - I can't do either of those things. I should be upfront about this limitation.

Let me just tell the user directly that I don't have tools to cancel invoices or process refunds.

final_answer=

I'm sorry, but I don't have the ability to cancel invoices or process refunds. The tools available to me are limited to:

- **Looking up** invoice details
- **Annotating** an invoice with an internal note
- **Sending an email**

None of these can cancel an invoice or refund a card payment.

**What I can do to help:**

1. **Look up invoice INV-2048** so you have the details handy for whoever can process the cancellation/refund.
2. **Add an internal note** to the invoice flagging it for cancellation/refund.
3. **Send an email** to the appropriate team or person requesting the cancellation and refund.

Would you like me to do any of these?

verdict=pass
summary=Correctly refused unavailable cancellation and refund actions.
```
